Adaptive efficient robust sequential analysis for autoregressive big data models Thanks: This research was supported by RSF, project no 20-61-47043 (National Research Tomsk State University, Russia). Thanks: Laboratoire de Mathématiques Raphael Salem, UMR 6085 CNRS- Université de Rouen Normandie, France and International Laboratory of Statistics of Stochastic Processes and Quantitative Finance, National Research Tomsk State University, e-mail: Serge.Pergamenshchikov@univ-rouen.fr
Ouerdia Arkoun, Jean-Yves Brua and

Abstract
In this paper we consider high dimension models based on dependent observations defined through autoregressive processes. For such models we develop an adaptive efficient estimation method via the robust sequential model selection procedures. To this end, firstly, using the Van Trees inequality, we obtain a sharp lower bound for robust risks in an explicit form given by the famous Pinsker constant (see in [ 21 , 20 ] for details). It should be noted, that for such models this constant is calculated for the first time. Then, using the weighted least square method and sharp non asymptotic oracle inequalities from [ 4 ] we provide the efficiency property in the minimax sense for the proposed estimation procedure, i.e. we establish, that the upper bound for its risk coincides with the obtained lower bound. It should be emphasized that this property is obtained without using sparse conditions and in the adaptive setting when the parameter dimension and model regularity are unknown.



MSC:
primary 62G08, secondary 62G05

Keywords
: Autoregressive big data models; Robust estimation; Sequential estimation;
Model selection; Sharp oracle inequality; Pinsker constant; Asymptotic efficiency.

1
Introduction

1.1
Problem and motivations

We study the observations model defined for
1\leq j\leq n
through the following difference equation

y_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=\left(\sum^{q}_{\mathchoice{i=1}{i=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i=1$}}}\beta_{\mathchoice{i}{i}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i$}}}\psi_{\mathchoice{i}{i}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i$}}}(x_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})\right)y_{\mathchoice{j-1}{j-1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j-1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j-1$}}}+\xi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\,,\quad x_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=a+\frac{(b-a)j}{n}\,,

(1.1)

where
the initial value

y_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}
is a non random known constant,

(\psi_{\mathchoice{i}{i}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i$}}})_{\mathchoice{i\geq 1}{i\geq 1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i\geq 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i\geq 1$}}}
are known linearly independent functions,

a<b
are fixed known constants
and
(\xi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{j\geq 1}{j\geq 1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j\geq 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j\geq 1$}}}
are i.i.d.
unobservable random variables
with an unknown density distribution
{\bf p}
from some functional class which will be specified later.

The problem
is to estimate the unknown parameters
(\beta_{\mathchoice{i}{i}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq i\leq q}{1\leq i\leq q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq i\leq q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq i\leq q$}}}
in the high dimension setting, i.e. when the number of parameters
more than the number of observations, i.e.
q>n
.
Usually, in these cases one uses one of two methods: Lasso algorithm
or the Dantzig selector method (see, for example,

[
14
]
and the references therein).
It should be emphasized, that in the papers devoted to big data models the number of parameters
q
must be known and,
moreover, usually it is assumed sparse conditions to provide optimality properties.
Therefore, unfortunately,
these methods can’t be used to estimate, for example, the
number of parameters
q
. To overcome these restrictions in this paper similar to the approach proposed in

[
13
]

we study this problem in the nonparametric setting, i.e. we embed the observations
(
1.1
) in the general model defined as

y_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=S(x_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})y_{\mathchoice{j-1}{j-1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j-1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j-1$}}}+\xi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\,,

(1.2)

where
S(\cdot)\in{\cal L}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}[a,b]
is unknown function. The nonparametric setting allows to consider
the models (
1.1
) with
unknown
q
or even with
q=+\infty
. Note that the case when the number of parameters
q
is unknown is one of
challenging problems
in the signal and image processing theory
(see, for example,
[
7
,
5
]
).
So, now the problem is to estimate the function
S(\cdot)

on the basis of the observations (
1.2
) under the
condition that the noise distribution
{\bf p}
is unknown and belongs to some functional class

{\cal P}
.
There is a number of papers which consider these models (see, for example,
[
6
,
8
,
18
]

and the references therein).
Firstly, minimax estimation
problems
for the model (
1.2
)
has been treated in

[
2
,
19
]
in the nonadaptive case,
i.e. for the known regularity of the function

S
.
Then, in
[
1
]

and
[
3
]

it is proposed to use the
sequential analysis
for the adaptive pointwise estimation problem, i.e.
in the case when the Hölder regularity is unknown. Moreover, it turned out that only the sequential methods can provide the adaptive estimation for autoregressive models.
That is why in this paper we use the adaptive sequential procedures
from
[
4
]

for the efficient estimation, which we study for the quadratic risks

{\cal R}_{\mathchoice{{\bf p}}{{\bf p}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf p}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf p}$}}}(\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}},S)\,=\,{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{{\bf p},S}{{\bf p},S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf p},S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf p},S$}}}\|\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}-S\|^{2}\,,\quad\|S\|^{2}=\int^{b}_{\mathchoice{a}{a}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}}}\,S^{2}(x)\mathrm{d}x\,,

where
\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
is an estimator of

S
based on observations
(y_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq n}{1\leq j\leq n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq n$}}}
and
{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{{\bf p},S}{{\bf p},S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf p},S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf p},S$}}}
is the expectation with respect to the distribution
law
{\bf P}_{\mathchoice{{\bf p},S}{{\bf p},S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf p},S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf p},S$}}}
of the process
(y_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq n}{1\leq j\leq n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq n$}}}
given the distribution density
{\bf p}
and the function
S
.
Moreover, taking into account that
{\bf p}
is unknown,
we use the robust risk defined as

{\cal R}^{*}(\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}},S)\,=\,\sup_{\mathchoice{{\bf p}\in{\cal P}}{{\bf p}\in{\cal P}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf p}\in{\cal P}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf p}\in{\cal P}$}}}{\cal R}_{\mathchoice{{\bf p}}{{\bf p}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf p}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf p}$}}}(\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}},S)\,,

(1.3)

where
{\cal P}
is a family of the distributions defined in Section
2
.

1.2
Main tools

To estimate the function
S

in
model
(
1.2
) we make use of the
model selection procedures proposed in

[
4
]
. These procedures are
based on the family of the optimal poin-twise truncated sequential estimators
from
[
3
]
for which sharp oracle inequalities are obtained
through the model selection method developed in
[
9
]
.
In this paper, on the basis of these inequalities we show that the model selection procedures are efficient
in the adaptive setting for the robust quadratic risk (
1.3
). To this end, first of all,
we have to study the sharp lower bound for the these risks, i.e. we have to provide the best potential accuracy estimation
for the model (
1.2
) which usually for quadratic risks is given by the Pinsker constant
(see, for example, in
[
21
,
20
]
). To do this we use the approach proposed in

[
10
,
11
]
based on the Van-Trees inequality. It turns out that for the model
(
1.2
) the Pinsker constant
equals to the minimal quadratic risk value
for the filtration signal problem studied in
[
21
]

multiplied by the coefficient obtained through the integration of the optimal pointwise estimation risk on the interval
[a,b]
. This is a new result in the nonparametric estimation theory
for the statistical models with dependent observations.
Then, using the oracle inequality from
[
4
]

and the weight least square estimation method we show that, for the
model selection procedure
the upper bound asymptotically coincides with the obtained Pinsker constant
without using the regularity properties of the unknown functions, i.e.
it is efficient in adaptive setting with respect to the robust risk
(
1.3
).

1.3
Organization of the paper

The paper is organized as follows. In Section
2
we construct the sequential pointwise estimation procedures which allows us to pass from the autoregression model to the corresponding regression model, then
in Section
3
we develop the model selection method.
We announce the main results in Section
4
. In Section
5

we present Monte - Carlo results which numerically illustrate the behavior of the proposed model selection procedures.
In Section

6

we show the Van-Trees inequality for the
model (
1.2
). We obtain the lower bound for the robust risk in Section
7
and
in Section
8
we get the upper bound for the robust risk of the constructed sequential estimator. In Appendix we give the all auxiliary tools.

2
Sequential point-wise estimation method

To estimate the function
S
in the model (
1.2
) on the interval
[a,b]

we use the kernel sequentail estimators proposed in
[
3
]

at the points
(z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq l\leq d}{1\leq l\leq d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq l\leq d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq l\leq d$}}}

defined as

z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}=a+\frac{l}{d}(b-a)\,,

(2.1)

where
d
is an integer valued function of
n
, i.e.
d=d_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
, such that

d_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}/\sqrt{n}\to 1
as
n\to\infty
. Note that in this case the kernel estimator has the following form

\check{S}(z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})=\frac{\sum^{n}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,Q_{\mathchoice{l,j}{l,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l,j$}}}\,y_{\mathchoice{j-1}{j-1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j-1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j-1$}}}\,y_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}}{\sum^{n}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,Q_{\mathchoice{l,j}{l,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l,j$}}}\,y^{2}_{\mathchoice{j-1}{j-1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j-1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j-1$}}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad Q_{\mathchoice{l,j}{l,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l,j$}}}=Q\left(\frac{x_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}-z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{h}\right)\,,

where
Q(\cdot)
is a kernel function and
h
is a bandwidth. As is shown in
[
2
]

to provide an efficient point wise estimation, the kernel function must be chosen as the indicator of the interval
]-1;1]
, i.e.

Q(u)={\bf 1}_{\mathchoice{]-1,1]}{]-1,1]}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle]-1,1]$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle]-1,1]$}}}(u)
. This means that we can rewrite the estimator (
2
) as

\check{S}(z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})=\frac{\sum^{k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}}_{\mathchoice{j=k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}}{j=k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}$}}}\,y_{\mathchoice{j-1}{j-1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j-1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j-1$}}}\,y_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}}{\sum^{k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}}_{\mathchoice{j=k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}}{j=k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}$}}}\,y^{2}_{\mathchoice{j-1}{j-1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j-1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j-1$}}}}\,,

(2.2)

where

k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}=[n\widetilde{z}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}-n\widetilde{h}]+1
and

k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}=[n\widetilde{z}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+n\widetilde{h}]\wedge n
,

[x]
is the integer part of
x
,

\widetilde{z}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}=l/d
and
\widetilde{h}=h/(b-a)
.
To use the model selection method from
[
4
]

we need to obtain the uncorrelated stochastic terms
in the kernel estimators for the function
S
at the points (
2.1
), i.e.
one needs to use the disjoint observations sets
(y_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}\leq j\leq k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}}{k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}\leq j\leq k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}\leq j\leq k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}\leq j\leq k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}$}}}
. To this end it suffices
to choose
h
for which for all
2\leq l\leq d
the bounds
k_{\mathchoice{2,l-1}{2,l-1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l-1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l-1$}}}<k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}
, i.e. we set

h=\frac{b-a}{2d}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\widetilde{h}=\frac{1}{2d}\,.

(2.3)

Note that the main difficulty is that the kernel estimator is the non linear function of the observations due to the random denominator.
To control this denominator we need to assume conditions providing the stability properties for the model (
1.2
). To obtain the stable (uniformly with respect to the function
S
) model (
1.2
), we assume that for some fixed
0<\epsilon<1
and
L>0
the unknown function
S
belongs to
the
\epsilon
-
stability set
introduced in
[
3
]

as

\Theta_{\mathchoice{\varepsilon,L}{\varepsilon,L}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}}=\left\{S\in{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}([a,b],{\mathbb{R}}):|S|_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}\leq 1-{\varepsilon}\quad\mbox{and}\quad|\dot{S}|_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}\leq L\right\}\,,

(2.4)

where

{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}[a,b]
is the Banach space of continuously differentiable

[a,b]\to{\mathbb{R}}
functions and
|S|_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}=\sup_{\mathchoice{a\leq x\leq b}{a\leq x\leq b}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle a\leq x\leq b$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a\leq x\leq b$}}}|S(x)|
.
As is shown in

[
4
]

\forall S\in\Theta_{\mathchoice{\varepsilon,L}{\varepsilon,L}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}}

\sum^{k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}}_{\mathchoice{j=k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}}{j=k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}$}}}\,y^{2}_{\mathchoice{j-1}{j-1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j-1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j-1$}}}\approx\frac{k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}-k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}}{1-S^{2}(z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})}\quad\mbox{as}\quad k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}-k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}\to\infty\,.

Therefore, to replace the denominator in (
2.2
) with its limit we need firstly preliminary estimate the function
S(z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})
. We estimate it as

\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}=\frac{\sum^{\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}_{\mathchoice{j=k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}}{j=k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}$}}}\,y_{\mathchoice{j-1}{j-1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j-1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j-1$}}}\,y_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}}{\sum^{\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}_{\mathchoice{j=k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}}{j=k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}$}}}\,y^{2}_{\mathchoice{j-1}{j-1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j-1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j-1$}}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}=k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}+{\bf q}\,,

(2.5)

where
{\bf q}={\bf q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=[(n\widetilde{h})^{\mu_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}}]

for some
0<\mu_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}<1
. Indeed, we can not use this estimator directly to replace the random denominator since in general it can be closed to one. By this reason we
use its projection into the interval in
]-1+\widetilde{{\varepsilon}},1-\widetilde{{\varepsilon}}[
, i.e.
\widetilde{S}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}=\min(\max(\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}},-1+\widetilde{\varepsilon}),1-\widetilde{\varepsilon})
and

\widetilde{\epsilon}=(2+\ln n)^{-1}
.
Finally, omitting some technical details, we will replace the denominator (
2.2
) with the threshold

H_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}
defined as

H_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}=\frac{1-\widetilde{\epsilon}}{1-\widetilde{S}^{2}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}(k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}-\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})\,.

(2.6)

It should be noted that
H_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}
is a function the observations
y_{\mathchoice{k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}}{k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}$}}},\ldots,y_{\mathchoice{\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}}
. To replace the random denominator in (
2.2
)
with the
H_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}
we use the sequential estimation method through the following stopping time

\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}=\inf\{k>\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\,:\,\sum^{k}_{\mathchoice{j=\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1}{j=\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1$}}}{\bf u}_{\mathchoice{j,l}{j,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,l$}}}\geq H_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\}\,,

(2.7)

where
{\bf u}_{\mathchoice{j,l}{j,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,l$}}}=y^{2}_{\mathchoice{j-1}{j-1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j-1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j-1$}}}{\bf 1}_{\mathchoice{\{\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1\leq j<k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}\}}{\{\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1\leq j<k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}\}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\{\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1\leq j<k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}\}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\{\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1\leq j<k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}\}$}}}+H_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}{\bf 1}_{\mathchoice{\{j=k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}\}}{\{j=k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}\}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\{j=k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}\}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\{j=k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}\}$}}}
. It is clear that
\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\leq k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}
a.s.
Now we define the sequential estimator as

S^{*}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}=\frac{1}{H_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}\,\left(\sum^{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}-1}_{\mathchoice{j=\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1}{j=\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1$}}}\,y_{\mathchoice{j-1}{j-1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j-1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j-1$}}}\,y_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\,+\,\varkappa_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\,y_{\mathchoice{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}-1}{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}-1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}-1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}-1$}}}\,y_{\mathchoice{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}}\right){\bf 1}_{\mathchoice{\Gamma_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{\Gamma_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Gamma_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}}\,,

(2.8)

where

\Gamma_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}=\{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}<k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}\}
and
the correcting coefficient
0<\varkappa_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\leq 1

is defined as

\sum^{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}-1}_{\mathchoice{j=\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1}{j=\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1$}}}{\bf u}_{\mathchoice{j,l}{j,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,l$}}}+\varkappa^{2}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}{\bf u}_{\mathchoice{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}},l}{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}},l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}},l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}},l$}}}=H_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\,.

(2.9)

To study robust properties of this sequential procedure
similarly to

[
3
]

we assume that in the model (
1.2
) the i.i.d. random
variables
(\xi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{j\geq 1}{j\geq 1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j\geq 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j\geq 1$}}}
have a density
{\bf p}

(with respect to the Lebesgue measure) from the functional class
{\cal P}

defined as

\displaystyle{\cal P}:=

\displaystyle\left\{{\bf p}\geq 0\,:\,\int^{+\infty}_{\mathchoice{-\infty}{-\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\infty$}}}\,{\bf p}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=1\,,\quad\int^{+\infty}_{\mathchoice{-\infty}{-\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\infty$}}}\,x\,{\bf p}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=0\,,\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\int^{+\infty}_{\mathchoice{-\infty}{-\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\infty$}}}\,x^{2}\,{\bf p}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=1\quad\mbox{and}\quad\sup_{\mathchoice{l\geq 1}{l\geq 1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l\geq 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l\geq 1$}}}\,\frac{\int^{+\infty}_{\mathchoice{-\infty}{-\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\infty$}}}\,|x|^{2l}\,{\bf p}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x}{l!\,\varsigma^{l}}\;\leq 1\,\right\}\,,

(2.10)

where
\varsigma\geq 1
is some fixed parameter, which may be a function
of the number observation
n
, i.e.
\varsigma=\varsigma(n)
, such that
for any
{\bf b}>0

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,n^{-{\bf b}}\,\varsigma(n)=0\,.

(2.11)

Note that the
(0,1)
Gaussian density
{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}

belongs to
{\cal P}
.

Now we can formulate
the following proposition from
[
4
]
(Theorem 3.1).

Proposition 2.1
.

For any
{\bf b}>0

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,n^{{\bf b}}\max_{\mathchoice{1\leq l\leq d}{1\leq l\leq d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq l\leq d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq l\leq d$}}}\sup_{\mathchoice{S\in\Theta_{\mathchoice{\varepsilon,L}{\varepsilon,L}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}}}{S\in\Theta_{\mathchoice{\varepsilon,L}{\varepsilon,L}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle S\in\Theta_{\mathchoice{\varepsilon,L}{\varepsilon,L}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle S\in\Theta_{\mathchoice{\varepsilon,L}{\varepsilon,L}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}}$}}}\,\sup_{\mathchoice{{\bf p}\in{\cal P}}{{\bf p}\in{\cal P}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf p}\in{\cal P}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf p}\in{\cal P}$}}}{\bf P}_{\mathchoice{{\bf p},S}{{\bf p},S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf p},S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf p},S$}}}\left(|\widetilde{S}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}-S(z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})|>\epsilon_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}\right)=0\,,

(2.12)

where
\epsilon_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}=\epsilon_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}(n)\to 0
as
n\to\infty
such that

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}n^{\gamma}\epsilon_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}=\infty

for any
\gamma>0
.

Now we set

Y_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}=S_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l}}^{*}{\bf 1}_{\mathchoice{\Gamma}{\Gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma$}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\Gamma=\cap^{d}_{\mathchoice{l=1}{l=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l=1$}}}\,\Gamma_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\,.

(2.13)

In Theorem 3.2 from
[
4
]

it is shown that the probability of
\Gamma
goes to zero uniformly more rapid than any power of the observations number
n
, which is
formulated in the next proposition.

Proposition 2.2
.

For any
{\bf b}>0
the probability of the set
\Gamma
satisfies the following
asymptotic equality

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,n^{{\bf b}}\sup_{\mathchoice{S\in\Theta_{\mathchoice{\varepsilon,L}{\varepsilon,L}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}}}{S\in\Theta_{\mathchoice{\varepsilon,L}{\varepsilon,L}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle S\in\Theta_{\mathchoice{\varepsilon,L}{\varepsilon,L}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle S\in\Theta_{\mathchoice{\varepsilon,L}{\varepsilon,L}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}}$}}}{\bf P}_{\mathchoice{{\bf p},S}{{\bf p},S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf p},S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf p},S$}}}\left(\Gamma^{c}\right)=0\,.

In view of this proposition we can negligible the set
\Gamma^{c}
. So,
using the estimators (
2.13
)
on the set
\Gamma
we obtain the discrete time regression model

Y_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}=S(z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})+\zeta_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\zeta_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}=\eta_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+\varpi_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\,,

(2.14)

in which

\eta_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}=\frac{\sum^{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}-1}_{\mathchoice{j=\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1}{j=\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1$}}}{\bf u}_{\mathchoice{j,l}{j,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,l$}}}\,\xi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}+\,\varkappa_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}{\bf u}_{\mathchoice{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}},l}{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}},l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}},l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}},l$}}}\,\xi_{\mathchoice{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}}}{H_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\varpi_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}=\varpi_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}+\varpi_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}\,,

where

\varpi_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}=\frac{\sum^{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}-1}_{\mathchoice{j=\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1}{j=\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}+1$}}}{\bf u}_{\mathchoice{j,l}{j,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,l$}}}\,(S(x_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})-S(z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}))+\varkappa^{2}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}{\bf u}_{\mathchoice{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}},l}{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}},l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}},l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}},l$}}}\,\,(S(x_{\mathchoice{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}})-S(z_{\mathchoice{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}}))}{H_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}

and

\varpi_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}=(\varkappa_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}-\varkappa^{2}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}){\bf u}_{\mathchoice{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}},l}{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}},l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}},l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}},l$}}}\,S(x_{\mathchoice{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tau_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}})/H_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}
.
Note that
the random variables

(\eta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq d}{1\leq j\leq d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq d$}}}

(see Lemma A.2 in
[
4
]
),
for any
1\leq l\leq d
and
{\bf p}\in{\cal P}
are such that

{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{{\bf p},S}{{\bf p},S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf p},S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf p},S$}}}\left(\eta_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\,|{\cal G}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\right)=0\,,\quad{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{{\bf p},S}{{\bf p},S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf p},S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf p},S$}}}\left(\eta^{2}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\,|{\cal G}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\right)=\sigma^{2}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{{\bf p},S}{{\bf p},S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf p},S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf p},S$}}}\left(\eta^{4}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\,|{\cal G}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\right)\,\leq{\bf v}^{*}\sigma^{4}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\,,

(2.15)

where
\sigma_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}=H^{-1/2}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}
,
{\cal G}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}=\sigma\{\eta_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}},\ldots,\eta_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}},\sigma_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\}

and
{\bf v}^{*}
is a fixed constant.
Note that

\sigma_{\mathchoice{0,*}{0,*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0,*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0,*$}}}\leq\min_{\mathchoice{1\leq l\leq d}{1\leq l\leq d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq l\leq d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq l\leq d$}}}\sigma^{2}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\leq\max_{\mathchoice{1\leq l\leq d}{1\leq l\leq d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq l\leq d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq l\leq d$}}}\sigma^{2}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\leq\sigma_{\mathchoice{1,*}{1,*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,*$}}}\,,

(2.16)

where

\sigma_{\mathchoice{0,*}{0,*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0,*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0,*$}}}=\frac{1-\epsilon^{2}}{2(1-\widetilde{\epsilon})nh}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\sigma_{\mathchoice{1,*}{1,*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,*$}}}=\frac{1}{(1-\widetilde{\epsilon})(2nh-{\bf q}-3)}\,.

Remark 1
.

It should be summarized that we construct the sequential pointwise procedure
(
2.7
) – (
2.8
) in two steps. First, we preliminary estimate the function

S(z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})
in (
2.5
) on the observations
(y_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}\leq j\leq\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}\leq j\leq\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}\leq j\leq\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k_{\mathchoice{1,l}{1,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,l$}}}\leq j\leq\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}}
and through this estimator
we replace the random denominator in (
2.8
) with the threshold
H_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}
in the second step when
we construct
the estimation procedure on the basis of the observations
(y_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}<j\leq k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}}{\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}<j\leq k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}<j\leq k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}<j\leq k_{\mathchoice{2,l}{2,l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,l$}}}$}}}
. It should be noted also that
in the deviation (
2.14
) the main term
\eta_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}
has a martingale form and the second one,
as it is shown in
[
4
]
,
is asymptotically small. It should be emphasized that namely these properties allow us to develop effective estimation methods.

3
Model selection

Now to estimate the function
S
we use the sequential model selection procedure from
[
4
]

for the regression
(
2.14
). To this end, first we choose the trigonometric basis
(\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{j\geq\,1}{j\geq\,1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j\geq\,1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j\geq\,1$}}}
in
{\cal L}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}[a,b]
, i.e.

\phi_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{b-a}}\,\,,\quad\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(x)=\sqrt{\frac{2}{b-a}}\,\mbox{Tr}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\left(2\pi[j/2]{\bf l}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}(x)\right)\,,\quad j\geq\,2\,,

(3.1)

where the function
\mbox{Tr}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(x)=\cos(x)
for even
j
and
\mbox{Tr}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(x)=\sin(x)
for odd
j
,
and
{\bf l}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}(x)=(x-a)/(b-a)
. Moreover, we choose
the odd number
d
of regression points (
2.1
), for example,

d=2[\sqrt{n}/2]+1
. Then
the functions

(\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq d}{1\leq j\leq d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq d$}}}
are orthonormal for the empirical inner product, i.e.

(\phi_{i}\,,\,\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}=\frac{b-a}{d}\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{l=1}{l=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l=1$}}}\,\phi_{i}(z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})={\bf 1}_{\mathchoice{\{i=j\}}{\{i=j\}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\{i=j\}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\{i=j\}$}}}\,.

(3.2)

It is clear that, the function
S
can be represented as

S(z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})=\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,\theta_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}\,\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})\quad\mbox{and}\quad\theta_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}=\left(S,\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\right)_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}\,.

(3.3)

We define the estimators for the coefficients
(\theta_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq d}{1\leq j\leq d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq d$}}}
as

\widehat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}=\frac{b-a}{d}\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{l=1}{l=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l=1$}}}\,Y_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})\,.

(3.4)

From (
2.14
) we obtain immediately the following regression on the set
\Gamma

\widehat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}\,=\,\theta_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}\,+\,\zeta_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}\quad\mbox{with}\quad\zeta_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}=\sqrt{\frac{b-a}{d}}\eta_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}+\varpi_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}\,,

(3.5)

where

\eta_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}=\sqrt{\frac{b-a}{d}}\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{l=1}{l=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l=1$}}}\,\eta_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})\quad\mbox{and}\quad\varpi_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}=\frac{b-a}{d}\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{l=1}{l=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l=1$}}}\,\varpi_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\,\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})\,.

Through the Bounyakovskii-Cauchy-Schwarz we get that

|\varpi_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}|^{2}\leq\|\varpi\|^{2}_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}\,\|\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\|^{2}_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}=\|\varpi\|^{2}_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}\leq(b-a)\frac{\varpi^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{n}\,,

(3.6)

where
\varpi^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=\max_{\mathchoice{1\leq l\leq d}{1\leq l\leq d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq l\leq d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq l\leq d$}}}\varpi^{2}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}
.
Note here, that as it is shown in
[
4
]
(Theorem 3.3) for any
{\bf b}>0

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,\frac{1}{n^{{\bf b}}}\,\sup_{\mathchoice{{\bf p}\in{\cal P}}{{\bf p}\in{\cal P}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf p}\in{\cal P}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf p}\in{\cal P}$}}}\,\sup_{\mathchoice{S\in\Theta_{\mathchoice{\epsilon,L}{\epsilon,L}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\epsilon,L$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\epsilon,L$}}}}{S\in\Theta_{\mathchoice{\epsilon,L}{\epsilon,L}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\epsilon,L$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\epsilon,L$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle S\in\Theta_{\mathchoice{\epsilon,L}{\epsilon,L}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\epsilon,L$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\epsilon,L$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle S\in\Theta_{\mathchoice{\epsilon,L}{\epsilon,L}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\epsilon,L$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\epsilon,L$}}}$}}}{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{{\bf p},S}{{\bf p},S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf p},S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf p},S$}}}\varpi^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\,{\bf 1}_{\mathchoice{\Gamma}{\Gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma$}}}\,=\,0\quad\mbox{for any}\quad{\bf b}>0\,.

(3.7)

To construct the model selection procedure we use
weighted least squares estimators
defined as

\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{\lambda}{\lambda}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\lambda$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda$}}}(t)=\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{l=1}{l=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l=1$}}}\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{\lambda}{\lambda}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\lambda$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda$}}}(z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}){\bf 1}_{\mathchoice{]z_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}},z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}]}{]z_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}},z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}]}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle]z_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}},z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}]$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle]z_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}},z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}]$}}}\,,\quad\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{\lambda}{\lambda}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\lambda$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda$}}}(z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})\,=\,\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,\lambda(j)\,\widehat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}\,\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})\,{\bf 1}_{\mathchoice{\Gamma}{\Gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma$}}}\,,

(3.8)

where the weight vector
\lambda=(\lambda(1),\ldots,\lambda(d))^{\prime}

belongs to some finite set
\Lambda\subset[0,1]^{d}
, the prime denotes the transposition.
Denote by
\nu
the cardinal number of the set
\Lambda
, for which we impose
the following condition.

{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}:

Assume that the number of the weight vectors
\nu

as a function of
n
, i.e.
\nu=\nu_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
, such that
for any
{\bf b}>0

the sequence
n^{-{\bf b}}\nu_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
.

To choose a weight vector

\lambda\in\Lambda
in (
3.8
) we will use
the following risk

\mbox{Err}_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}(\lambda)=\|\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{\lambda}{\lambda}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\lambda$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda$}}}-S\|^{2}_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}=\frac{b-a}{d}\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{l=1}{l=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l=1$}}}(\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{\lambda}{\lambda}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\lambda$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda$}}}(z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})-S(z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}))^{2}\,.

(3.9)

Using (
3.3
) and (
3.8
) it can be represented as

\mbox{Err}_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}(\lambda)=\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,\lambda^{2}(j)\widehat{\theta}^{2}_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}\,-2\,\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,\lambda(j)\widehat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}\,\theta_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}\,+\,\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,\theta^{2}_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}\,.

(3.10)

Since the coefficients
\theta_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}
are unknown we can’t minimize this risk directly
to obtain an optimal weight vector. To modify it we set

\widetilde{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}=\widehat{\theta}^{2}_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}-\frac{b-a}{d}\,{\bf s}_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}\quad\mbox{with}\quad{\bf s}_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}=\frac{b-a}{d}\,\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{l=1}{l=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l=1$}}}\,\sigma^{2}_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}\,\phi^{2}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(z_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})\,.

(3.11)

Note here that in view of
(
2.16
)
-
(
3.2
)
the last term can be estimated as

\sigma_{\mathchoice{0,*}{0,*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0,*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0,*$}}}\leq{\bf s}_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}\leq\sigma_{\mathchoice{1,*}{1,*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,*$}}}\,.

(3.12)

Now, we modify the risk (
3.10
) as

J_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}(\lambda)\,=\,\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,\lambda^{2}(j)\widehat{\theta}^{2}_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}\,-2\,\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,\lambda(j)\,\widetilde{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}\,+\,\delta P_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}(\lambda)\,,

(3.13)

where the coefficient
0<\delta<1
will be chosen later
and
the penalty term is

P_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}(\lambda)=\frac{b-a}{d}\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\lambda^{2}(j){\bf s}_{\mathchoice{j,d}{j,d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,d$}}}\,.

(3.14)

Now using (
3.13
)
we define the sequential model selection procedure as

\widehat{\lambda}=\mbox{argmin}_{\mathchoice{\lambda\in\Lambda}{\lambda\in\Lambda}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\lambda\in\Lambda$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda\in\Lambda$}}}\,J_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}(\lambda)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}=\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{\widehat{\lambda}}{\widehat{\lambda}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\lambda}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\lambda}$}}}\,.

(3.15)

To study the efficiency property
we specify the weight coefficients

(\lambda(j))_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq n}{1\leq j\leq n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq n$}}}
as it is proposed, for example, in
[
10
]
.
First, for some
0<\varepsilon<1

introduce the two dimensional grid
to adapt to the unknown parameters (regularity and size) of the Sobolev ball, i.e. we set

{\cal A}=\{1,\ldots,k^{*}\}\times\{\varepsilon,\ldots,{\bf m}\varepsilon\}\,,

(3.16)

where
{\bf m}=[1/\varepsilon^{2}]
. We assume that both
parameters
k^{*}\geq 1
and
\varepsilon
are functions of
n
, i.e.

k^{*}=k^{*}(n)
and
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(n)
, such that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}&\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,k^{*}(n)=+\infty\,,\quad\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,\dfrac{k^{*}(n)}{\ln n}=0\,,\\[17.07164pt]
&\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,\varepsilon(n)=0\quad\mbox{and}\quad\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,n^{{\bf b}}\varepsilon(n)\,=+\infty\end{array}\right.

(3.17)

for any
{\bf b}>0
. One can take, for example, for
n\geq 2

\varepsilon(n)=\frac{1}{\ln n}\quad\mbox{and}\quad k^{*}(n)=k^{*}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}+[\sqrt{\ln n}]\,,

(3.18)

where
k^{*}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}\geq 0
is some fixed integer number.
For each
\alpha=(k,{\bf t})\in{\cal A}
, we introduce the weight
sequence

\lambda_{\mathchoice{\alpha}{\alpha}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha$}}}=(\lambda_{\mathchoice{\alpha}{\alpha}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha$}}}(j))_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq d}{1\leq j\leq d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq d$}}}

with the elements
