\lambda_{\mathchoice{\alpha}{\alpha}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha$}}}(j)={\bf 1}_{\mathchoice{\{1\leq j<j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}\}}{\{1\leq j<j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}\}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\{1\leq j<j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}\}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\{1\leq j<j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}\}$}}}+\left(1-(j/\omega_{\alpha})^{k}\right)\,{\bf 1}_{\mathchoice{\{j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}\leq j\leq\omega_{\mathchoice{\alpha}{\alpha}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha$}}}\}}{\{j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}\leq j\leq\omega_{\mathchoice{\alpha}{\alpha}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha$}}}\}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\{j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}\leq j\leq\omega_{\mathchoice{\alpha}{\alpha}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha$}}}\}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\{j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}\leq j\leq\omega_{\mathchoice{\alpha}{\alpha}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha$}}}\}$}}}\,,

(3.19)

and

\omega_{\mathchoice{\alpha}{\alpha}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha$}}}=\omega_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}+(b-a)^{2k/(2k+1)}\left(\frac{(k+1)(2k+1)}{\pi^{2k}k}{\bf t}\,n\right)^{1/(2k+1)}\,.

Here,
j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}
and
\omega_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}
are such that
j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}\to\infty
,

j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}=\mbox{o}\left((n/\varepsilon)^{1/(2k+1)}\right)
and
\omega_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}=\mbox{O}(j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}})
as
n\to\infty
.
In this case we set

\Lambda\,=\,\{\lambda_{\mathchoice{\alpha}{\alpha}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha$}}}\,,\,\alpha\in{\cal A}\}
.
Note, that these weight coefficients are
used in
[
16
,
17
]

for continuous time regression
models to show the asymptotic efficiency.
It will be noted that in this case the cardinal of the set
\Lambda
is

\nu=k^{*}m
. It is clear that the properties (
3.17
)
imply the condition
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}
.
In
[
4
]
we showed the following result.

Theorem 3.1
.

Assume that the conditions (
2.11
)
and

{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}

hold.
Then for any

n\geq\,3
, any

S\in\Theta_{\mathchoice{\varepsilon,L}{\varepsilon,L}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}}
and any
0<\delta\leq 1/12
,
the procedure (
3.15
) with the coefficients (
3.19
)
satisfies the following oracle inequality

{\cal R}^{*}(\widehat{S}_{*},S)\leq\frac{(1+4\delta)(1+\delta)^{2}}{1-6\delta}\min_{\mathchoice{\lambda\in\Lambda}{\lambda\in\Lambda}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\lambda\in\Lambda$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda\in\Lambda$}}}{\cal R}^{*}(\widehat{S}_{\lambda},S)+\,\frac{{\bf B}^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\delta n}\,,

(3.20)

where the term
{\bf B}^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
is such that

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,n^{-{\bf b}}{\bf B}^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=0
for any
{\bf b}>0
.

Remark 2
.

In this paper we will use the inequality (
3.20
) to study efficiency properties for the model selection procedure
(
3.15
) with the weight coefficients
(
3.19
) in adaptive setting, i.e. in the case when the regularity of the function
S
(
1.2
) is unknown.

4
Main results

First, to study the minimax properties for the estimation problem for the model (
1.2
)
we need to introduce some functional class. To this end
for any fixed

{\bf r}>0
and
{\bf k}\geq 2

we set

{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}=\left\{f\in\Theta_{\mathchoice{\varepsilon,L}{\varepsilon,L}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}}:\,\sum_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}^{+\infty}\,{\bf a}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\,\theta^{2}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\leq{\bf r}\right\}\,,

(4.1)

where

{\bf a}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=\sum^{{\bf k}}_{\mathchoice{l=0}{l=0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l=0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l=0$}}}\,(2\pi[j/2]/(b-a))^{2l}
,

(\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{j\geq 1}{j\geq 1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j\geq 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j\geq 1$}}}
are the trigonometric Fourier coefficients in
{\cal L}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}[a,b]
, i.e.

\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=(f,\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})=\int^{b}_{\mathchoice{a}{a}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}}}f(x)\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(x)\mathrm{d}x

and
(\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{j\geq 1}{j\geq 1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j\geq 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j\geq 1$}}}
is the trigonometric basis defined in (
3.1
).
It is clear that we can represent this functional class as the Sobolev ball

{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}=\left\{f\in\Theta_{\mathchoice{\varepsilon,L}{\varepsilon,L}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}}:\,\sum_{\mathchoice{j=0}{j=0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=0$}}}^{{\bf k}}\,\|f^{(j)}\|^{2}\leq{\bf r}\right\}\,.

Now, for this set we define the normalizing coefficients

{\bf l}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k}}{{\bf k}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k}$}}}({\bf r})={\bf l}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k}}{{\bf k}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k}$}}}({\bf r})\,=\,((1+2{\bf k})r)^{1/(2{\bf k}+1)}\,\left(\frac{{\bf k}}{\pi({\bf k}+1)}\right)^{2{\bf k}/(2{\bf k}+1)}

and

\varsigma_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}=\varsigma_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}(S)=\int_{\mathchoice{a}{a}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}}}^{b}(1-S^{2}(u))\mathrm{d}u\,.

(4.2)

It is well known that
in regression models with the functions

S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}

the minimax convergence rate is

n^{-2{\bf k}/(2{\bf k}+1)}
(see, for example,
[
10
,
15
]

and the references therein). Our goal in this paper is to show the same property for the non parametric auto-regressive models
(
1.2
). First we have to obtain a lower bound for the risk (
1.3
) over all possible
estimators
\Xi_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
, i.e. any measurable function of the observations
(y_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}},\ldots,y_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}})
.

Theorem 4.1
.

For the model (
1.2
)
the robust risk (
1.3
) normalized by the coefficient
\upsilon(S)=((b-a)\varsigma)^{-2{\bf k}/(2{\bf k}+1)}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}

can be estimated from below as

\liminf_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\inf_{\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\in\Xi_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}\,n^{2{\bf k}/(2{\bf k}+1)}\sup_{\mathchoice{S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}}{S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}$}}}\,\upsilon(S){\cal R}^{*}(\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}},S)\,\geq{\bf l}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k}}{{\bf k}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k}$}}}({\bf r})\,.

(4.3)

Now to study the procedure (
3.15
) we have to add some condition on the penalty coefficient
\delta
which provides sufficiently small
penalty term in (
3.13
).

{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}
:
Assume that the parameter
\delta
is a function of
n
, i.e.
\delta=\delta_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
such that

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\delta_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=0
and

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,n^{-{\bf b}}\delta_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=0

for any
{\bf b}>0
.

Theorem 4.2
.

Assume that the conditions
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}
–
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}
hold.
Then the model selection procedure

\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}
defined in
(
3.15
) with the weight vectors (
3.19
)
admits the following asymptotic upper bound

\limsup_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,n^{2{\bf k}/(2{\bf k}+1)}\sup_{\mathchoice{S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}}{S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}$}}}\,\upsilon(S)\,{\cal R}(\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}},S)\,\leq{\bf l}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k}}{{\bf k}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k}$}}}({\bf r})\,.

Now
Theorems

4.1
-
4.2
imply the following efficiency property.

Corollary 4.1
.

Assume that the conditions
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}
–
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}
hold.
The model selection procedure

\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}
defined in
(
3.15
) and (
3.19
)
is efficient, i.e.

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\frac{\inf_{\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\in\Xi_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}\,\sup_{\mathchoice{S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}}{S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}$}}}\,\upsilon(S)\,{\cal R}^{*}(\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}},S)}{\sup_{\mathchoice{S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}}{S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}$}}}\,\upsilon(S){\cal R}(\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}},S)}=1\,.

(4.4)

Moreover,

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,n^{2{\bf k}/(2{\bf k}+1)}\sup_{\mathchoice{S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}}{S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}$}}}\,\upsilon(S)\,{\cal R}^{*}(\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}},S)\,={\bf l}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k}}{{\bf k}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k}$}}}({\bf r})\,.

(4.5)

Remark 3
.

Note that the limit equalities (
4.4
) and
(
4.5
) imply that the function

{\bf l}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k}}{{\bf k}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k}$}}}({\bf r})/\upsilon(S)

is the
minimal value of the normalized asymptotic quadratic robust risk, i.e.
the Pinsker constant
in this case. We remind that the coefficient
{\bf l}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k}}{{\bf k}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k}$}}}({\bf r})

is the well known Pinsker constant for the ”signal+standard white noise” model obtained in
[
21
]
.
Therefore,
the Pinsker constant for the model (
1.2
)
is represented by the Pinsker constant for the
”signal+white noise” model in which the noise intensity is given by
the function
(
4.2
).

Now we assume that in the model (
1.2
) the functions
(\psi_{\mathchoice{i}{i}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i$}}})_{\mathchoice{i\geq 1}{i\geq 1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i\geq 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i\geq 1$}}}
are
orthonormal in
{\cal L}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}[a,b]
, i.e.

(\psi_{\mathchoice{i}{i}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i$}}},\psi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})={\bf 1}_{\mathchoice{\{i=j\}}{\{i=j\}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\{i=j\}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\{i=j\}$}}}
.
We use the estimators (
3.8
)
to estimate the parameters
\beta=(\beta_{\mathchoice{i}{i}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i$}}})_{\mathchoice{i\geq 1}{i\geq 1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i\geq 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i\geq 1$}}}
as

\widehat{\beta}_{\mathchoice{\lambda}{\lambda}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\lambda$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda$}}}=(\widehat{\beta}_{\mathchoice{\lambda,i}{\lambda,i}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\lambda,i$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda,i$}}})_{\mathchoice{i\geq 1}{i\geq 1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i\geq 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i\geq 1$}}}

and

\widehat{\beta}_{\mathchoice{\lambda,i}{\lambda,i}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\lambda,i$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda,i$}}}=(\psi_{\mathchoice{i}{i}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i$}}},\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{\lambda}{\lambda}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\lambda$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda$}}})
.
Then, similarly we use the selection model procedure (
3.15
)
as

\widehat{\beta}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}=(\widehat{\beta}_{\mathchoice{*,i}{*,i}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*,i$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*,i$}}})_{\mathchoice{i\geq 1}{i\geq 1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i\geq 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i\geq 1$}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\widehat{\beta}_{\mathchoice{*,i}{*,i}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*,i$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*,i$}}}=(\psi_{\mathchoice{i}{i}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i$}}},\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}})\,.

(4.6)

It is clear, that in this case

|\widehat{\beta}_{\mathchoice{\lambda}{\lambda}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\lambda$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda$}}}-\beta|^{2}=\sum^{\infty}_{\mathchoice{i=1}{i=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i=1$}}}(\widehat{\beta}_{\mathchoice{\lambda,i}{\lambda,i}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\lambda,i$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda,i$}}}-\beta_{\mathchoice{i}{i}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i$}}})^{2}=\|\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{\lambda}{\lambda}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\lambda$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda$}}}-S\|^{2}

and

|\widehat{\beta}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}-\beta|^{2}=\|\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}-S\|^{2}
.

Note, that Theorem
3.1
implies that the estimator (
4.6
)
is optimal in the sharp oracle inequality sense which is established in the following theorem.

Theorem 4.3
.

For any

S\in\Theta_{\mathchoice{\varepsilon,L}{\varepsilon,L}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varepsilon,L$}}}
,

n\geq\,3
and
0<\delta\leq 1/12
,

{\cal R}^{*}(\widehat{\beta}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}},\beta)\leq\,\frac{(1+4\delta)(1+\delta)^{2}}{1-6\delta}\,\min_{\mathchoice{\lambda\in\Lambda}{\lambda\in\Lambda}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\lambda\in\Lambda$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda\in\Lambda$}}}{\cal R}^{*}(\widehat{\beta}_{\mathchoice{\lambda}{\lambda}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\lambda$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda$}}},\beta)+\frac{{\bf B}^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\delta n}\,,

where

{\cal R}^{*}(\widehat{\beta},\beta)=\sup_{\mathchoice{{\bf p}\in{\cal P}}{{\bf p}\in{\cal P}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf p}\in{\cal P}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf p}\in{\cal P}$}}}{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{{\bf p},S}{{\bf p},S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf p},S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf p},S$}}}|\widehat{\beta}-\beta|^{2}
and

{\bf B}^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
satisfies the limit property mentioned in Theorem
3.1
.

Note now, that
Theorems

4.1
and
4.2

imply the efficiency property for the estimate (
4.6
) based on
the model selection procedure
(
3.15
)
constructed with
the penalty threshold
\delta
satisfying the
condition
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}
.

Theorem 4.4
.

Then the estimate (
4.6
)
is asymptotically efficient, i.e.

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,n^{2{\bf k}/(2{\bf k}+1)}\,\sup_{\mathchoice{S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}}{S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}$}}}\,\upsilon(S){\cal R}^{*}(\widehat{\beta}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}},\beta)=l_{\mathchoice{k}{k}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}({\bf r})

and

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\frac{\inf_{\mathchoice{\widehat{\beta}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\in\Xi_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\widehat{\beta}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\in\Xi_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\beta}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\in\Xi_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\beta}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\in\Xi_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}}\,\sup_{\mathchoice{S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}}{S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}$}}}\,\upsilon(S){\cal R}^{*}(\widehat{\beta}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}},\beta)}{\sup_{\mathchoice{S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}}{S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}$}}}\,\upsilon(S){\cal R}^{*}(\widehat{\beta}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}},\beta)}=1\,,

(4.7)

where
\Xi_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
is the set estimators for
\beta
based on the observations
(y_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq n}{1\leq j\leq n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq n$}}}
.

Remark 4
.

It should be noted that we obtained the efficiency property (
4.7
) for the big data autoregressive model (
1.2
)
without using the parameter dimension
q
or sparse conditions usually used for such models (see, for example, in

[
14
]
).

5
Monte - Carlo simulations

In this section we present the numeric results obtained through the Python soft
for the model (
1.2
) in which
(\xi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq n}{1\leq j\leq n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq n$}}}
are i.i.d.
{\cal N}(0,1)
random variables and
0\leq x\leq 1
, i.e.
a=0
and
b=1
.
In this case we simulate the
model selection procedure (
3.15
) with the weights (
3.19
) in which

k^{*}=150+\sqrt{\ln n}
,
{\bf m}=[\ln^{2}n]
,
\varepsilon=1/\ln n
. Moreover, the parameters
j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}
and

\omega_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}
are chosen as

j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}=\frac{\underline{\omega}}{200+\ln\underline{\omega}}\,,\qquad\underline{\omega}=\ln n+\left(\frac{(k+1)(2k+1)}{\pi^{2k}k}{\bf t}\,n\right)^{1/(2k+1)}

and
\omega_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}=j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}+\ln n
.
First we study the model (
1.2
) with

S_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}(x)=0,5\cos(2\pi x)
and then for the function

S_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}(x)=0,1+\sum^{q}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,\frac{\cos(2\pi jx)}{(j+3)^{2}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad q=100000\,.

d=2[\sqrt{n}/2]+1
points in (

).

Then we calculate the empiric risks as

\overline{{\cal R}}=\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\widehat{{\bf E}}\left(\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(z_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})-S(z_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})\right)^{2}\,,

(5.1)

where the expectation is taken as an average over
M=50
replications, i.e.

\widehat{{\bf E}}\left(\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(.)-S(.)\right)^{2}=\frac{1}{M}\sum_{\mathchoice{l=1}{l=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l=1$}}}^{M}\left(\widehat{S}^{l}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(\cdot)-S(\cdot)\right)^{2}\,.

We use also the relative risk

\overline{{\cal R}}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}=\frac{\overline{{\cal R}}}{\|S\|^{2}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\|S\|^{2}_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}^{n}S^{2}(x_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})\,.

(5.2)

Remark 5
.

From numerical simulations of the procedure (

)
with various observation durations

and for the different functions

we may conclude that the quality of the estimation improves as the number of observations increases.

6
The van Trees inequality

In this section we consider the nonparametric autoregressive model (
1.2
)
with the
(0,1)
gaussian i.i.d. random variable

(\xi_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq l\leq n}{1\leq l\leq n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq l\leq n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq l\leq n$}}}
and the parametric linear
function
S
, i.e.

S_{\mathchoice{\theta}{\theta}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\theta$}}}(x)=\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\,\psi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(x)\,,\quad\theta=(\theta_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}},\ldots,\theta_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}})^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\,.

(6.1)

We assume that the functions
(\psi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq d}{1\leq j\leq d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq d$}}}
are orthogonal with respect to the scalar product
(
3.2
). Let now
{\bf P}^{n}_{\mathchoice{\theta}{\theta}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\theta$}}}
be the distribution in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
of the observations
y=(y_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}},\ldots,y_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}})

in the model (
1.2
)
with the function (
6.1
)
and
\nu^{n}_{\mathchoice{\xi}{\xi}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\xi$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\xi$}}}
be the distribution
in

{\mathbb{R}}^{n}

of the gaussian vector

(\xi_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}},\dots,\xi_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}})
.
In this case the Radon - Nykodim density is given as

f_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(y,\theta)=\frac{\mathrm{d}{\bf P}^{(n)}_{\mathchoice{\theta}{\theta}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\theta$}}}}{\mathrm{d}\nu^{n}_{\mathchoice{\xi}{\xi}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\xi$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\xi$}}}}=\exp\left\{\sum^{n}_{\mathchoice{l=1}{l=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l=1$}}}S_{\mathchoice{\theta}{\theta}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\theta$}}}(x_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})y_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}}y_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}-\frac{1}{2}\sum^{n}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}S^{2}_{\mathchoice{\theta}{\theta}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\theta$}}}(x_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})y^{2}_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}}\right\}\,.

(6.2)

Let
\varrho
be a prior distribution density on
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
for the parameter
\theta
of
the following form

\varrho(\theta)=\prod_{j=1}^{d}\varrho_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})
,
where
\varrho_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}
is a some probability density in
{\mathbb{R}}
with continuously derivative
\dot{\varrho}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}
for which the Fisher information is finite, i.e.

I_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=\int_{\mathchoice{{\mathbb{R}}}{{\mathbb{R}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathbb{R}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbb{R}}$}}}\,\frac{\dot{\varrho}^{2}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(z)}{\varrho_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(z)}\,\mathrm{d}z<\infty\,.

(6.3)

Let
g(\theta)
be a continuously differentiable
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\to{\mathbb{R}}
function such that

\lim_{\mathchoice{|\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}|\to\infty}{|\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}|\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle|\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}|\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle|\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}|\to\infty$}}}\,g(\theta)\,\varrho_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})=0\quad\mbox{and}\quad\int_{\mathchoice{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathbb{R}}^{d}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbb{R}}^{d}$}}}\,|g^{\prime}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(\theta)|\,{\bf u}(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}\theta<\infty\,,

(6.4)

where
g^{\prime}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(\theta)=\,\partial g(\theta)/\partial\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}
.

For any
Q({\mathbb{R}}^{n+d})
-measurable integrable function
H=H(y,\theta)
we denote

\widetilde{{\bf E}}\,H=\int_{\mathchoice{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+d}}{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+d}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathbb{R}}^{n+d}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbb{R}}^{n+d}$}}}\,H(y,\theta)\,f_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(y,\theta)\,\varrho(\theta)\mathrm{d}\nu^{(n)}_{\mathchoice{\xi}{\xi}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\xi$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\xi$}}}\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,.

(6.5)

Let
{\cal F}^{y}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
be the field generated by the observations (
1.2
), i.e.

{\cal F}^{y}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=\sigma\{y_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}},\ldots,y_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\}
.
Now we study the Gaussian the model (
1.2
) with the function
(
6.1
).

Lemma 6.1
.

For any
{\cal F}^{y}_{n}
-measurable square integrable
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
function
\widehat{g}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}

and for any
1\leq j\leq d
,
the mean square accuracy of the function
g(\cdot)

with respect to the distribution (
6.5
)
can be estimated from below as

\widetilde{{\bf E}}(\widehat{g}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}-g(\theta))^{2}\geq\frac{\overline{g}^{2}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}}{\widetilde{{\bf E}}\,\Psi_{\mathchoice{n,j}{n,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n,j$}}}+I_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}}\,,

(6.6)

where
\Psi_{\mathchoice{n,j}{n,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n,j$}}}=\sum^{n}_{\mathchoice{l=1}{l=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l=1$}}}\,\psi^{2}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(x_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})\,y^{2}_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}}
and

\overline{g}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=\int_{\mathchoice{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathbb{R}}^{d}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbb{R}}^{d}$}}}\,g^{\prime}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(\theta)\,\varrho(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}\theta
.

Proof.

First, for any
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
we set

\widetilde{U}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=\widetilde{U}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(y,\theta)=\frac{1}{f_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(y,\theta){\bf u}(\theta)}\,\frac{\partial\,(f_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(y,\theta){\bf u}(\theta))}{\partial\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}}\,.

Taking into account the condition (
6.4
) and
integrating by parts we get

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\bf E}}

\displaystyle\left((\widehat{g}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}-g(\theta))\widetilde{U}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\right)=\int_{\mathchoice{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+d}}{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+d}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathbb{R}}^{n+d}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbb{R}}^{n+d}$}}}\,(\widehat{g}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(y)-g(\theta))\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}}\left(f_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(y,\theta){\bf u}(\theta)\right)\mathrm{d}\theta\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{(n)}_{\mathchoice{\xi}{\xi}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\xi$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\xi$}}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathchoice{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+d-1}}{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+d-1}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathbb{R}}^{n+d-1}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbb{R}}^{n+d-1}$}}}\left(\int^{+\infty}_{\mathchoice{-\infty}{-\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\infty$}}}\,g^{\prime}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(\theta)\,f_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(y,\theta){\bf u}(\theta)\mathrm{d}\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\right)\left(\prod_{\mathchoice{i\neq j}{i\neq j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i\neq j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i\neq j$}}}\mathrm{d}\theta_{i}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{(n)}_{\mathchoice{\xi}{\xi}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\xi$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\xi$}}}=\overline{g}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\,.

Now by the Bouniakovskii-Cauchy-Schwarz inequality
we obtain the following lower bound for the quadratic risk

\widetilde{{\bf E}}(\widehat{g}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}-g(\theta))^{2}\geq\overline{g}^{2}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}/\widetilde{{\bf E}}\widetilde{U}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}^{2}
.
To study the denominator in this
inequality note that in view of the representation
(
6.2
)

\frac{1}{f_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(y,\theta)}\frac{\partial\,f_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(y,\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}}=\sum^{n}_{\mathchoice{l=1}{l=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l=1$}}}\,\psi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(x_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})\,y_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}}(y_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}-S_{\mathchoice{\theta}{\theta}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\theta$}}}(x_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})y_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}})\,.

Therefore, for each
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
,

{\bf E}^{(n)}_{\mathchoice{\theta}{\theta}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\theta$}}}\,\frac{1}{f_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(y,\theta)}\frac{\partial\,f_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(y,\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}}\,=0

and

{\bf E}^{(n)}_{\mathchoice{\theta}{\theta}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\theta$}}}\,\left(\frac{1}{f_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(y,\theta)}\frac{\partial\,f_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(y,\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}}\right)^{2}=\,{\bf E}^{(n)}_{\mathchoice{\theta}{\theta}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\theta$}}}\sum^{n}_{\mathchoice{l=1}{l=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l=1$}}}\,\psi^{2}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(x_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})\,y^{2}_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}}=\,{\bf E}^{(n)}_{\mathchoice{\theta}{\theta}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\theta$}}}\,\Psi_{\mathchoice{n,j}{n,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n,j$}}}\,.

Using the equality

\widetilde{U}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=\frac{1}{f_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(y,\theta)}\frac{\partial\,f_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(y,\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}}+\frac{1}{{\bf u}(\theta)}\frac{\partial\,{\bf u}(\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}}\,,

we get

\widetilde{{\bf E}}\,\widetilde{U}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}^{2}=\,\widetilde{{\bf E}}\,\Psi_{\mathchoice{n,j}{n,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n,j$}}}+\,I_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}
.
Hence
Lemma
6.1
.

∎

7
Lower bound

First, taking into account that the
(0,1)
gaussian density
{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}

belongs to the class (
2.10
), we get

{\cal R}^{*}(\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}},S)\,\geq\,{\cal R}_{\mathchoice{{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}}{{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}$}}}(\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}},S)
.
Now, according to the general lower bounds methods (see, for example, in
[
10
]
)
one needs to estimate this risk from below by some bayesian risk and, then to apply the van Trees inequlity.
To define the bayesian risk we need to choose a prior distribution on
{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}
. To this end, first
for any vector
\kappa=(\kappa_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq d}{1\leq j\leq d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq d$}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
,
we set

S_{\mathchoice{\kappa}{\kappa}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kappa$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kappa$}}}(x)=\sum_{j=1}^{d_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}\,\kappa_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\,\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(x)\,,

(7.1)

where
(\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq d_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{1\leq j\leq d_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq d_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq d_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}}
is the trigonometric basis defined in (
3.1
).
We will choose a prior distribution on the basis of
the optimal coefficients in (
7.1
) (see, for example, in
[
20
,
21
]
)
providing asymptotically the maximal valuer for the risk
{\cal R}_{\mathchoice{{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}}{{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}$}}}(\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}},S)
over
S
. So, we
choose it as the distribution
\mu_{\mathchoice{\kappa}{\kappa}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kappa$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kappa$}}}
in
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
of the random vector

\kappa=(\kappa_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq d_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{1\leq j\leq d_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq d_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq d_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}}
defined by its components as

\kappa_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=s_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\,\eta^{*}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\,,

(7.2)

where
\eta^{*}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}
are i.i.d. random variables with the continuously differentiable density
\rho_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(\cdot)
defined in
Lemma
A.1
with
{\bf N}=\ln n
and
n>e^{2}
,

s_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=\sqrt{\frac{(b-a)s^{*}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}}{n}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad s^{*}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\,=\left(\frac{d_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{j}\right)^{{\bf k}}-1\,.

To choose the number of the terms
d
in (
7.1
) one needs to keep this function in
{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}
, i.e. one needs to provide
for arbitrary fixed
0<\rho<1

the following property

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}{\bf a}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\,s^{2}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=\rho{\bf r}:={\bf r}_{\mathchoice{\rho}{\rho}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rho$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rho$}}}\,.

(7.3)

To do this we set

d=d_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=\left[{\bf g}^{*}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k}}{{\bf k}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k}$}}}\,n^{1/(2{\bf k}+1)}\right]\,,

(7.4)

where
{\bf g}^{*}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k}}{{\bf k}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k}$}}}=(b-a)^{(2{\bf k}-1)/(2{\bf k}+1)}{\bf l}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k}}{{\bf k}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k}$}}}({\bf r}_{\mathchoice{\rho}{\rho}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rho$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rho$}}})({\bf k}+1)/{\bf k}

and

{\bf l}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k}}{{\bf k}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k}$}}}({\bf r}_{\mathchoice{\rho}{\rho}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rho$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rho$}}})=\,((1+2{\bf k}){\bf r}_{\mathchoice{\rho}{\rho}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rho$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rho$}}})^{1/(2{\bf k}+1)}\,\left(\frac{{\bf k}}{\pi({\bf k}+1)}\right)^{2{\bf k}/(2{\bf k}+1)}=\rho^{1/(2{\bf k}+1)}{\bf l}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k}}{{\bf k}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k}$}}}({\bf r})\,.

It is clear that almost sure the function (
7.1
) can be bounded as

\max_{\mathchoice{a\leq x\leq b}{a\leq x\leq b}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle a\leq x\leq b$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a\leq x\leq b$}}}\left(|S_{\mathchoice{\kappa}{\kappa}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kappa$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kappa$}}}(x)|+|\dot{S}_{\mathchoice{\kappa}{\kappa}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kappa$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kappa$}}}(x)|\right)\leq{\bf c}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}\frac{\ln n}{\sqrt{n}}\sum^{d_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}j\left(\frac{d_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{j}\right)^{{\bf k}/2}:=\delta^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\,,

(7.5)

where

{\bf c}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}=\sqrt{2}(b-a+\pi)/(b-a)^{3/2}
.
Note, that
\delta^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
for
{\bf k}\geq 2
.
Therefore, for sufficiently large
n
the function (
7.1
)
belongs to the class (
2.4
). Now,
\forall f\in{\cal L}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}[a,b]
, we denote by
{\bf h}(f)
its projection
in
{\cal L}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}[a,b]

onto the ball

{\cal W}^{*}_{\mathchoice{{\bf r}}{{\bf r}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf r}$}}}=\{f\in{\cal L}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}[a,b]\,:\,\|f\|\leq{\bf r}\}
, i.e.

{\bf h}(f)=({\bf r}/\max({\bf r}\,,\,\|f\|))f
.
Since
{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}\subset{\cal W}^{*}_{\mathchoice{{\bf r}}{{\bf r}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf r}$}}}
, then for
S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}
we have

\|\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}-S\|^{2}\geq\|\widehat{{\bf h}}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}-S\|^{2}
,
where

\widehat{{\bf h}}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}={\bf h}(\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}})
.
Therefore, for large
n

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathchoice{S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}}{S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}$}}}\,\upsilon(S)\,{\cal R}_{\mathchoice{{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}}{{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}$}}}(\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}},S)\,

\displaystyle\geq\,\int_{\mathchoice{{\bf D}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{{\bf D}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf D}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf D}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}}\,\upsilon(S_{\mathchoice{z}{z}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle z$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z$}}}){\bf E}_{\mathchoice{{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}},S_{\mathchoice{z}{z}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle z$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z$}}}}{{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}},S_{\mathchoice{z}{z}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle z$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}},S_{\mathchoice{z}{z}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle z$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}},S_{\mathchoice{z}{z}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle z$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z$}}}$}}}\|\widehat{{\bf h}}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}-S_{\mathchoice{z}{z}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle z$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z$}}}\|^{2}\,\mu_{\kappa}(\mathrm{d}z)

\displaystyle\geq\,\upsilon_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}\,\int_{\mathchoice{{\bf D}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{{\bf D}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf D}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf D}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}}\,{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}},S_{\mathchoice{z}{z}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle z$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z$}}}}{{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}},S_{\mathchoice{z}{z}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle z$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}},S_{\mathchoice{z}{z}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle z$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}},S_{\mathchoice{z}{z}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle z$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z$}}}$}}}\|\widehat{{\bf h}}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}-S_{\mathchoice{z}{z}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle z$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z$}}}\|^{2}\,\mu_{\kappa}(\mathrm{d}z)\,,

(7.6)

where

{\bf D}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=\left\{z\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\,:\,\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,{\bf a}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}z^{2}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\leq{\bf r}\right\}

and

\upsilon_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}=\inf_{\mathchoice{|S|_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}\leq\delta^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{|S|_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}\leq\delta^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle|S|_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}\leq\delta^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle|S|_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}\leq\delta^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}}\,\upsilon(S)
.
Note that

\upsilon_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}\to(b-a)^{-4{\bf k}/(2{\bf k}+1)}
as

n\to\infty
.
Using the distribution
\mu_{\mathchoice{\kappa}{\kappa}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kappa$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kappa$}}}
we introduce
the following Bayes risk as

\widetilde{{\cal R}}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}(\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}})=\widetilde{{\bf E}}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}\,\|\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}-S_{\mathchoice{z}{z}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle z$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z$}}}\|^{2}=\int_{\mathchoice{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathbb{R}}^{d}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbb{R}}^{d}$}}}{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}},S_{\mathchoice{z}{z}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle z$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z$}}}}{{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}},S_{\mathchoice{z}{z}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle z$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}},S_{\mathchoice{z}{z}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle z$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}},S_{\mathchoice{z}{z}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle z$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z$}}}$}}}\,\|\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}-S_{\mathchoice{z}{z}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle z$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z$}}}\|^{2}\,\mu_{\mathchoice{\kappa}{\kappa}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kappa$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kappa$}}}(\mathrm{d}z)\,.

(7.7)

Now taking into account that
\|\widehat{{\bf h}}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\|^{2}\leq r
,
we get

\sup_{\mathchoice{S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}}{S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle S\in{\cal W}_{\mathchoice{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{{\bf k},{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf k},{\bf r}$}}}$}}}\,\upsilon(S)\,{\cal R}_{\mathchoice{{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}}{{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}$}}}(\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}},S)\,\geq\,\upsilon_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}\,\widetilde{{\cal R}}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}(\widehat{{\bf h}}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}})-2\,\upsilon_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}\,{\bf R}_{\mathchoice{0,n}{0,n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0,n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0,n$}}}

(7.8)

and

{\bf R}_{\mathchoice{0,n}{0,n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0,n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0,n$}}}=\int_{\mathchoice{{\bf D}^{c}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{{\bf D}^{c}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf D}^{c}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf D}^{c}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}}\,\,(r+\|S_{\mathchoice{z}{z}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle z$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z$}}}\|^{2})\,\mu_{\mathchoice{\kappa}{\kappa}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kappa$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kappa$}}}(\mathrm{d}z)=\int_{\mathchoice{{\bf D}^{c}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{{\bf D}^{c}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf D}^{c}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bf D}^{c}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}}\,\left({\bf r}+\,|z|^{2}\right)\,\mu_{\mathchoice{\kappa}{\kappa}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kappa$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kappa$}}}(\mathrm{d}z)
.
Note here that this term is studied In
Lemma
A.3
. Moreover,
note also that for any
z\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}

we get

\|\widehat{{\bf h}}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}-S_{z}\|^{2}\geq\sum_{j=1}^{d_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}\,(\widehat{z}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}-z_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})^{2}

and

\widehat{z}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=\left(\widehat{{\bf h}}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\,,\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\right)
. Therefore, from Lemma
6.1

with
g(\theta)=\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}
it follows, that for any
1\leq j\leq d

and any
{\cal F}^{y}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
measurable random variable
